up infidel as to the personal kingship of 
Christ, as many professing Christians are now, 
but not infidel as to the outward Christian 
church; and this distinction seems necessary 
in order to obviate the apparent difficulty of 
VOL. II. H 


90 


considering the kings of. Christendom as in- 
fidels, or heathen. We learn farther from 
_ Zech. xiv. 3,4; Joel iii. 16; and Isa. xxv. 9, 
that there will be a manifestation at this time 
of Christ in their favour; and in all probability 
there will be a series of miraculous interposi- 
tions as there was at their exodus from Egypt. 
The issue of this battle, and the result from it, 
will be, that by its means all the enemies of 
the Lord and of the Jews will be destroyed ; 
this will be effected by fire from heaven, as 
well as by the civil war which Anastasius pointed 
out to us. There will be no human help to 
Christ; according as it is written of him, “I 
have trodden the wine-press alone, and of the 
people there was none with me,—and I looked 
and there was none to help,” &c. Isa. Ixiii. 
3, 5, 6. This will produce repentance and con- 
version in the whole nation, or at least all that 
part of them who shall be at Armageddon, and 
subsequently of the rest, and the establishment 
of Christ’s kingdom upon the earth; and one, 
and perhaps the most awful circumstance at- 
tending it, will be, that not one individual 
throughout Christendom shall escape, even as not 
a single individual escaped at the Deluge. 
The purposes to be effected by the war are, 
amongst other important things, the verifying 
of the word of God; as many of the circum- 
stances in the life of Christ were performed 
principally because they were written of him. 
By this also the righteous judgments of God 
will be vindicated, and the terrible example 


91 


will produce the conversion of the world; “ for 
when thy judgments are in the earth, the inha- 
bitants of the world will learn righteousness.” 
Isa. xxvi. 9. This will be the last battle, after 
which wars shall cease, and swords shall be 
beat into ploughshares, and peace and righ- 
teousness shall flourish on the earth.

Thraso.—Harvest is a figure of united ‘mercy

very few gaining the victory over a large army,

after which Melchizedek appears’to the conque-

the Jews will mourn over the death of Messiah, 
whom their fathers had pierced long before. 
This mourning is on the day of atonement, or 
fast. There are three things to be specially 
noted in the day of atonement. 1. The goat which 
was killed, representing Christ’s death, and 
which has a continuous application. 2. The 
scape goat, which represents the mourning of 
the Jews on coming to apply the benefit of that 
death, and have the sins of the congregation 
taken away. 3. The High Priest coming out 
of the Holy of Holies, having changed his 
robes and put on the shining garments, can 
represent nothing but Christ coming out of 
heaven in glory, without the sins of the people.

Eugenio.—The chronology of Gideon’s victory 
may be gathered from the fact, that at the time 
he was called he was found threshing; which 
shews that it. was subsequent to the harvest, 
as the vintage is. 

Isocrates.—It is admitted, on all hands, that 
the coming up out of Egypt, and taking pos- 
session of. the promised land, under Joshua, 
is typical of the Jews coming up out of their 
present bondage, and taking possession. of 
Canaan in perpetuity, under the antitype of 
Joshua, the Lord Jesus Christ. This being 
the case, it is natural. to refer to the history 
of those events, and without going back. further 
than Joshua, it will be found that the whole 
sequence of acts recorded of him, are spoken 
of in other parts of Scripture, as to receive their 
antitype in the war of Armageddon. The chief 
of these events are seven. 1. They renewed 
their covenant with God. 2. Blowing of, the 
trumpets of jubilee, or ram’s horn. 3. Jericho 
fell; which is illustrative of their taking pos- 
session of the land. 4. Being in security, they 
receive an. unexpected discomfiture at Ai. 
5. The Lord instructs’ them in the cause of 
this, and immediately Ai falls. 6. The Gi- 
beonites confederate. 7. Confederacy of kings 
with excision. Thus it is not any one event 
which constitutes a perfect type, but the 
whole of the events taken together form. it; 
this is the case frequently with typical persons, 
where two or more must be joined together, in 
order to form a complete type of the Lord Jesus 
Christ, in some single character. After. the 


95. 


conclusion of all. these events, Joshua reminds 
the people that: no-one thing had failed them -of 
all the good things the Lord: had promised them.

of the valley of Megiddon ?

scription of it; ‘this plain—called by way 
of eminence the great plain, in Scripture, and’ 
elsewhere, the great plain, or field, of Esdraelon 
—the field: of: Megiddon, the Galilean plain— 
we found one vast meadow, covered with the 
richest pasture.—It has been a chosen place 
for encampment, in every contest: carried on- 
in: this country, from the days of Nabucho- 
donosor, king: of. Assyria, unto the disastrous 
march of Napoleon Buonaparte from Egypt’ 
into. Syria. Jews, Gentiles, Saracens, Chris- 
tian: Crusaders, and Anti-christian Frenchmen, 
Egyptians, Persians, Druses, Turks, and Arabs, 
warriors of: every nation that is under heaven, 
have pitched their tents on the plain of Es- 
draelon; and have beheld the banners of: their 
nations. wet with the dew of Tabor and: Her- 
mon.” With respect to the period when the 
battle is to be fought, it is of importance to 
mark it: historically. It- makes little difference 
whether the word zodzuoc be translated’ battle 
or war, since every war. terminates by some 
great and decisive battle; it is therefore pro- 
bable that there will be a season of warfare 
terminating in this last signal act: of vengeance. 
Much obseurity upon :what is revealed concern: 


96 


ing it will be removed, if it shall appear that 
there will be a partial restoration of the Jews 
previous to the general establishment of the 
whole nation. It seems from Isa. lxv. and lxvi., 
as well as from Ezek. xxxviii. and xxxix., that 
some of those who are at first restored will be 
a part of the fullness of Christ, of his true 
mystical body, the natural branches graffed 
in again. Since the world will not be able 
to see the invisible saints, it is necessary that 
the Jews should be restored, in order that the 
righteous judgments of God may be manifested . 
to the world; as the visible and invisible 
churches are always running parallel to each 
other. It is not to be expected that any people 
will be on the side of the Jews, for it is always 
God’s plan to produce great events by the most 
unlikely means, and in this point lies the force 
of the types which have been named; such. 
as Gibeon, Jehoshaphat, &c. It is of importance 
to consider the purpose of this battle; it is not 
the last, but the last act but one, and next but 
one to the highest, of the great drama of time, 
which was to manifest to creature, that God 
is the alone I am; to the mutable, that it is 
He alone “that changeth not;” because he 
here will shew by the weak, that He is the 
Micuty One, and this battle is a testimony _ 
to the Unseen One. A future dispensation 
will be opened, in which God will be manifested 
as the ALL Goop ONE.

points out Paul as a type of the conversion 
of the national Israel; there is also a great 
similarity to be observed in their conduct and 
that of Thomas. It is absolutely necessary 
that the same being should be revealed in power 
and glory, who has been seen by them before 
in weakness and humiliation. One of the most 
extraordinary circumstances which is revealed 
as attendant upon the return of the Jews to 
their land, is the great wealth which it is said 
shall accompany them. This appears to be 
the motive for the attack which shall be made 
upon them in their own land, Ezek. xxxviii. 
10,11; Mic. iv. 13. It is a curious fact, that 
many learned Jews say that Hag. ii. 6, 7, does 
not belong to the second temple, but to a third. 
Aristo.—In considering in detail the circum- 
stances of which the battle consists, it is well 
to reflect on the causes which shall have con- 
duced to it in the dispensations of God, and 
on what the conduct of the nations has been, 
to call for so tremendous a judgment. It ap- 
pears clear, from various passages of Holy Writ, 
I. That the oppression of the Jews is one of 
the most heinous sins for which we Gentiles 
have to pay a reckoning. II. That the Gentile 
church has not fulfilled the great purpose for 
which it was established. III. That the Chris- 
tian Sovereignties, or Temporal Rulers, have 
failed in their purpose also. Ist. In Zech. i. 
14—21, we have a vision, of the Lord speak- 
ing “ comfortable words” concerning Jerusalem 
“Thus saith the Lord of Hosts, I am jealous 


98 


far. Jerusalem, and for Zion, with. a great jea- 
lousy, and I am very sore displeased with the 
heathen that are at ease, for I was but a 
little displeased, and they helped forward the 
affliction,” &c.; from which. whole passage- we 
gather, that the persecution which the Jews 
have undergone at the hands of the Gentiles, 
is one great moving cause with the Lord, to 
bring them to account. The horn of a beast 
is that in which its strength resides, and hence 
the horn is- a symbol of power: the four horns 
in the subsequent verses, are, doubtless. the 
four Gentile Monarchies of: Daniel, who have 
been the chief oppressors of God’s people; 
whilst the four artificers of v. 20, represent the 
agency by which those oppressors are requited. 
Joshua and Zerubbabel, taken. together, form 
the compound type of the Lord, who alone, in 
his own person, unites the offices of king, and 
builder of his temple. That Babylon, or the Papal 
Apostacy, is the last of these powers, is abun- 
dantly clear from Isaiah xlvii., in which. the 
same language is applied to the former, which, 
in the Apocalypse, is applied to the latter. 
The lxxxiiid Psalm contains the greatest num- 
ber of types of any passage in Scripture, de- 
seriptive of the last: judgment at Armageddon ; 
the special cause of this is therein assigned, 
that the nations have taken counsel against 
God’s people, and consulted against his hidden 
ones, saying, “ let us cut them off from being 
a nation, that the name of Israel may be no 
more in remembrance.” Certainly the conduct 


N 


99. 


which has been exhibited towards Israel, at 
the hands of the Gentiles, is a sufficient cause. 
for a similar judgment overtaking them. The 
world: was indebted to Israel by all the ties 
of gratitude, for the knowledge of: the one true 
God, of: its standing in His sight, and of the 
mode of access to his favor. This knowledge 
the Gentiles took at the hands of the Jews, 
but. forgot those from wham they. had received: 
it, and trampled them under foot; so that their 
iniquity was composed of the greatest possible 
ingratitude and cruelty, and therefore it is 
righteous that they should be judged. In al- 
most every Psalm in which judgment on the 
nations. is spoken of, the cause assigned is 
the oppression of the Jews. From Is. xl. to l. 
the Jews are spoken of as God’s witnesses to 
national. ingratitude; and Paul warns the Gen- 
tiles in Rom. xi. that they shall be cast off for 
the same sins which produced the rejection 
of the Jews. 

2d. But the chief cause of the judgment: 
which is to be poured out at Armageddon, is. 
the apostacy of. the Christian Church; for 
while the judgment on. the nations is only 
mentioned as. consequent upon the ‘other, Ba- 
bylon herself. is the principal object. Dan. 
vii. proves this beyond a doubt. The only two 
emblematical personages, which are represented. 
as destroyed before the second death, are the 
beast. and the false prophet, which shews that 
the grand monument to future times of the 
battle, is the destruction of the kingdoms of 


100 


this earth, and the apostate church. The 
Jewish church, with her gifts of temple-ser- 
vice, Levitical priesthood, sacrifices, rites, pro- 
phecies, and types, was set for the testimony 
of the Messiah: the Christian church, by her 
water of baptism, and especially by her chief 
ordinance, “ the foolishness of preaching,” was 
set for the testimony of the Spirit. In the 
latter, all carnal elements were taken away, 
and there is no holy thing visible on earth, no 
-transubstantiation. This dispensation is wholly 
Spiritual: the fulness of the Holy Spirit shone 
In Christ, and a portion of it was poured out 
at Pentecost. The sin of the Jewish church 
was sin against the Son of Man, which may be 
pardoned by the cross; the sin of the Gentile 
church is sin against the Holy Ghost, which 
is committed by her again adopting those carnal 
elements, and so covering with confusion, and 
rejecting, that manifestation of the Holy Spirit, 
which Christ gave from heaven in those or- 
dinances; and this is the reason why she 
has no season for repentance. The church has 
resisted the testimony of the Spirit in the chil- 
dren of God, and been unfaithful to her own 
ordinances; therefore the beast is immediately 
cast into the fire. The sin of the Jewish church 
having been committed against the Son of Man, 
it shall be forgiven, though not in this age: but 
the sin of the Gentile apostacy having been com- 
mitted against the Holy Ghost, (for the Christian 
dispensation is the ministration of the Spirit, 
2 Cor.ii.7,8,) it shall not be forgiven either in this

3d. The lxxiid Psalm represents the rebellion 
of the temporal rulers, the mighty ones, judges, 
and kings of the earth, against the King of 
kings. To understand the sin which the king- 
doms have committed, it is necessary to see 
the new condition into which they were put 
by Christ. The gospel of Christ was the first 
revelation from God, that Cæsar had rights 
as well as God, and which gave to kings, as 
such, their high standing, by putting His people 
under them; (Rom. xiii. 1—7; 1 Tim. ii. 1; 
1 Pet. ii. 13—17 ;) for under the Old Testament 
dispensation the king of Judah was a part of 
the church, and as such was entitled to ho- 
mage: thus establishing that divine right in 
kings, (which term they have much abused 
and perverted, by using it against the divine 
laws of righteous government,) for renouncing 
of which, this country will yet suffer shipwreck. 
Though the kings would not know this, yet 
from that moment they were constituted His 
viceroys. Therefore they were invested by the 
preaching of the Gospel ; they received that unc- 
tion which is the only true anointing. For 
what end? Ist. That they might receive the 
homage of His own people; this gave them their 
dignity. 2dly. He laid down a law by which 
they should govern themselves: the Gospel con- 
tained this law; here was their honor and dignity 
under Christ: they were brought into a moral 
Capacity for being judged, the law being given to 


102 


them by which they should be judged. Deliver- 
ance to the sinner, and blessing to the state, went 
pari passu with the preaching of the Gospel. But 
the kings, instead of being honest vicegerents for 
another, compelled Christ’s people to bow down 
to themselves. The viceroy was himself to go- 
vern, according to the law of the Gospel, viz. : 
to protect the poor and needy, the fatherless 
and him that had no helper. They were, there- 
fore, in all this investiture of dominion, moral 
agents, capable of judging and of being judged. 
The great majority of the nations’ rejected this 
gospel dignity. It has been preached in the 
rest of the world, and every state which has 
received it has been exalted like a city set 
upon a hill. That there was abundant time 
for it to have spread every where, had the- 
people felt an inclination towards it, is fully 
proved by the rapidity with which that stinking 
herb tobacco, spread from America to the utmost 
confines of the earth, within a few years of its 
first discovery. This then constitutes the first 
count of the indictment against the nations 
of the world. Here was one part, the Roman 
empire, which had been appointed to receive it. 
The Emperors consented to take their standing 
under Christ, by the setting up of an established 
religion. By doing this, the princes brought 
their people into the condition of being judged 
by the king, Christ. They by degrees renounced 
the dignity which Christ had given them of 
ruling over the saints, and put it under the feet 
of the church, instead of maintaining it in 


103 


their own persons. The moment this was done, 
their guilt was complete, and they destroyed 
the ordinance of God, which had-declared that 
his church should be under the powers of this 
world; for they ceased to be representatives of 
his kingly office, for which alone they were 
appointed ; it was good for the church to be 
put under such subjection, that smarting under 
the viceroy, she might long for the king, and 
that she might testify for righteousness to the 
king, as the prophets did to the Jewish church. 
Kings are not necessarily tyrants, but placed 
in great temptation, and therefore needing great 
grace. It was good for the kings, that the 
church should bear testimony for righteousness, 
and thus might be a proper monitor to them, 
and to all other magistrates, of the way in 
which they should fulfil their duty. But when 
the kings, who were representatives of Christ’s 
kingly office, as the church was of his prophetic 
office, forgot their duty to Him; the king be- 
came an apostate king, as the church became 
an apostate church. 

Hence a book, the A E is written 
chiefly about the kingly office, to shew to the 
church the arts of Satan, by which the king- 
doms would become apostate; and these are 
the counts upon which the judgment of Arma- 
geddon proceeds. The chief type which illus- 
trates the kingly apostacy, is to be found in 
the history of Jehu, Jonadab, and Jezebel, of 
which the latter, we know by Rev. ii. 20, is the 
Papacy; for a type is the language of events’ 


104 


‘past, predicting events to come’; or facts written 
out in the language of past events. 

. Anastasius.—The strongest instance of the / 
apostacy of temporal rulers, is to be found 
in the West Indies, where laws, instead of 
being made to protect the natural, unalien- 
able, and personal rights of every human 
creature against arbitrary power, are made to 
prevent man from using those gifts which 
God has given him for His service, and to 
rivet their chains of ignorance and suffering 
for the private emolument of their worse than 
Egyptian taskmaster. In. this country too, 
we have evangelical political economists, en- 
couraging the repeal of the only. laws which 
afford subsistence to the destitute, pervert- 
ing the smattering of science, with which 
they are acquainted, to the increased op- 
pression of: the poor, the needy, and the 
defenceless. Such must. be the necessary ten- 
dency of the study of all the applied sciences, 
when that study is not based upon the written 
will of God: such must be the fruit of the 
London University, not in political economy 
alone, but in legislation, in ethics, in = 
and in politics.

fraud, robbery, violence, oppression, and ini- 
quity, at all comparable to that which is main- 
tained in the Colonies, and which the English 
Government might instantly ameliorate if it 
‘pleased :. and doubtless there will be a day

flee to the refuge in Christ Jesus, which is still

the earth preparatory to the day of Armageddon ?

Christendom, similarto that which was pronounced 
on Israel in Isa. vi. The Prophet there receives 
the awful commission to “ go and tell the people, 
Hear ye indeed, but understand not; and see 
ye indeed, but perceive not; make the heart of 
this people fat, and make their ears heavy, and 
shut their eyes ; lest they see with their eyes, 
and hear with their ears, and understand with 
their heart, and convert, and be healed:” to

-he speaks in parables, Matt. xiij. 10—16. In

the last judgment upon Egypt, to which I have

in the midst thereof; and I will destroy the 
counsel thereof: and they shall seek to the idols, 
and to the charmers, and to them that have 
familiar spirits, and to the wizards.——The

~-——-Providence himself will intervene, 

To throw his dark displeasure o’er the scene. — 

All are his instruments; each form of war, 

What burns at home, or threatens from afar, 
Nature in arms, her elements at strife, 

The storms that overset the joys of life, 

Are but his rods to scourge a guilty land, 

And waste it at the bidding of his hand. 

He gives the word, and mutiny soon roars 

In all her gates, and shakes her distant shores; 
The standards of all nations are unfurl’d ; 

She has one foe, and that one foe the world. 

And if he mark that people with a frown, 

And mark them with a seal of wrath pressed down, 
Obduracy takes place; callous and tough, 

The reprobated race grows judgment proof : | 
Earth shakes beneath them, and heaven roars above, 
But nothing scares them from the course they love. 
To the lascivious pipe, and wanton song, 

That charm down fear, they frolic it along, 

With mad rapidity and unconcern, 

Down to the gulph, from which is no return. 

They trust in navies, and their navies fail— 

God’s curse can cast away ten thousand sail. 

They trust in armies, and their courage dies: 

In wisdom, wealth, in fortune, and in lies: 

But all they trust in withers, as it must, 


When HE commands IN WHOM THEY PLACE NO TRUST.

Thales.—It was a saying of King James, that 
kingcraft. was a matter above the ken of a sub- 
ject; and there is much truth in the axiom ; for 
in no instance are, or can be, the ruled fit 
judges of the motives which actuate aruler. If 
we discern this amongst ourselves, with what 
infinitely greater force does the principle 
apply to the judgments of Almighty God to- 
wards man. The fallen creature is so steeped 
in unholiness, that his tendency is always to- 
wards it, and the holiest man which the world 
ever saw, could form no adequate notion of the 
holiness of God: hence we every where find in 
Scripture that man thinks himself more mer- 
ciful than God; the bias of his mind being to 
pass over sin as a thing of comparatively little 
importance: witness, for example, Abraham’s 
intercession for Sodom; Jeremiah; and others: 
whereas, the angels, who partake more of the 
holiness of that Nature and Power which pre- 
serves them sinless, are represented as being 
more jealous for holiness than mercy, more 
anxious for justice than for pardon.

v. Destruction of the nations of Canaan. 
Here we have the fall of Jericho, produced by 
blasts of priests’ trumpets, to which the fall of 
the papal tyranny over the conscience, by the 
dissemination of the Word, has often been 
compared; and particularly the overthrow of 
{srael’s enemies in two great battles. The first 
against the nations leagued against Gibeon. The 
sun stood still on Gibeon while they were de- 
stroyed. Gibeon was a Gentile city, where the 
Tabernacle was during the whole of David's 
reign, 2 Chron. i. 3. Gibeon is a type of that 
church which contains the true witness in these 
latter times, which the Sun of Righteousness 
defends, but whose confessors Rome persecutes, 
and whose blood calls for vengeance against 
Babylon. Two circumstances are striking. 
1. Hail is a chief instrument of destruction, 
Josh. x. 11; Rev. xvi. 21. 2. Joshua smote all 
the country, and utterly destroyed all that 
breathed, Isa. xiii. 19, 20. The second battle

was against nations more numerous and for- 
midable, and Gibeon not concerned. 1. Five 
kings in the first battle, and thirteen or fourteen 
tribes united in the second. 2. They fought 
against Israel, as Gog will. 3. Their chariots 
were burned, as Gog’s chariots and weapons 
will be, Nahum i. 13; Ps. xlvi. 9. 4. None left 
alive, Zech. xiv. 5. No city of the land made 
peace with Israel, save Gibeon, as only the 
elect among the Gentiles will be preserved, 
besides Israel. 6. Afterwards the land rested 
from war. : 

vi. Victories of Barak and Deborah over Si- 
sera, and of Gideon over the Midianites. This 
type is clearly seen by reference to Ps. xxxiii., 
where nations are enumerated such as never yet 
confederated, and where the Assyrian is said to 
help or be an arm to the children of Lot. (1.) Over 
Jabin’s army, the type of Babylon, the victory 
was obtained; 1. by Barak (p3, lightning) for 
as the lightning, &c. so shall the coming of the 
Son of man be; the Lord shall destroy Babylon 
with the brightness of his coming: 2. by Deborah, 
(the spouse of the Word,) the church calling on 
the Lord to avenge her, (Rev. vi. 10; Luke xviii.7.) 
as Deborah on Barak: 3. by Jael, not of Israel, 
yet a chief actor. These victories are, 1. Over 
Sisera, Jabin’s general, who is the principal 
person introduced. He mightily oppressed Israel 
with his chariots of iron, as the Papacy wields 
the artillery of persecution, and is principally 
noticed in the judgment. 2. A family supposed 
to be at peace with him destroyed him, as those

speak of the desolations attending the last judg- 
ments. The king of the Amalekites was spared - 
by Saul, but afterwards slain; so the Pope will 
be spared by the kings of the earth, but meet 
his doom in conjunction with the rest of Anti- 
christ at a subsequent period.

Jews will, be. the. instruments, in God’s hands,.

‘Mristo.-By attentively considering Rev. xvi. 
it will appear that the Jewish people are the 
persons there denominated kings of the east.. 
In the original it is kings from the sun risings. 
The kings of the earth, and of the whole world, 
mentioned also, may mean those who rule over 
the earth and world; but the construction will 
not admit of it in the case before us, which 
cannot mean the kings who reign in the east. 
It designates no more than the quarter from 
whence they come, and not the territory over 
which they rule. The question next arises, 
with what propriety are the tribes of Israel 
denominated kings from the sun risings? and 
why are they not called by their proper name 
of Israel? First, for the same reason that 
Rome is called Babylon, and the Turkish 
Empire Euphrates ; because it is a symbolical 
prophecy, written with a symbolical, and not 
with a literal language, in order to cast a veil 
over it, and to connect it with the events of 
Old Testament history, from which the lan- 
guage is taken. Secondly, that the names of 
the ten tribes had already been used as the 
symbol of a Christian nation which was sepa- 
rated from the apostacy of the: ten-horned 
beast, and defended from the judgment of the 
vials; which nation I believe to be the British 
nation, which stands related to apostate Chris- ` 
tendom, just as the twelve tribes stood related 
to the apostacy. of the primitive religion. ‘The. 


118 


proper name of the tribes having been thus 
appropriated as a symbol, it was necessary, 
as well to prevent confusion, as to preserve 
the symbolical character of the book, that 
another name should be chosen for a symbol 
of the tribes, which is the one before us, and 
for which many reasons may be advanced. 
Zechariah’s prophecying of Christ, upon the 
circumcision of the Baptist, calls him the day- 
spring, or, rising from on high. Peter, carrying 
the minds of the disciples forward to the second 
coming of the Lord, calls it the dawning of the 
day, and the arising of the day-star; in Apoc. vii. 
the angel which seals the tribes ascends from 
the rising of the sun, and these kings whose |. 
way 1s prepared, are from the risings of the sun. 
All these passages have reference to the same 
great event of the second advent of the Lord, 
expressed in Mal. iv. 2, by the rising of the Sun 
of Righteousness; of which rising all the pro- 
phets give particular accounts, the source of 
which symbol is clearly seen in Isa. lx. 3; 
“The Gentiles shall come to thy light, and 
kings to the brightness of thy rising.” This is 
spoken of Christ and his Jerusalem, for they 
are not to be separated; and there is a dis- 
tinction between the Gentiles and the kings, 
the latter of whom are said to come to the 
brightness of his rising, and in Rev. xvi. we 
have the Holy Land cleared to make a way 
‘for these kings from the risings of the sun. His 
rising is to be in the east, and his own people 
come to its brightness. Those who come to 


119 


the brightness of his rising are called kings 
in Isaiah. We ought not to wonder then at 
their being called kings in the Apocalypse. 
But observe, in confirmation of this, at ver. 10, 
“the sons of strangers shall build up thy walls, 
and their kings shall minister unto thee,’ — 
“that men may bring unto thee the forces of 
the Gentiles, and that the kings may be 
brought; for the nation and king that will not 
serve thee shall perish.” Again xlix. 23, “kings 
shall be thy nursing fathers, and queens thy 
nursing mothers, they shall bow down with 
their faces towards the earth, and lick up the 
dust of thy feet.” This style of prophecy be- 
longs to those who shall be rulers of kings, and 
who therefore may, with all propriety, be them- 
selves styled kings. But that there may be 
no doubt left on this subject, I refer you to 
Psalm xlv., -which treats of the marriage of the 
great king with the Jewish church, where it is 
said of him, that kings’ daughters were amongst 
his honourable women, to attend upon his queen, 
who standeth at his right hand in gold of Ophir. 
And, further, it is said unto her, “Instead of 
thy fathers, shall be thy children, whom thou 
mayest make princes in all the earth ;” and in 
Ps. Ixviii., which describes the glorious jubilee 
of the returning tribes, it is thus written, “ there 
is little Benjamin with their ruler, the princes of 
Judah, and their council the princes of Zebulon, 
and the princes of Naphtali;” and this is the 
style in which the Lord addresses them the 
first time he spake to them by the mouth of

Moses, after they came out of Egypt, saying, 
Exod. xix. 8, “ now, therefore, if ye will obey 
my voice indeed, and keep my covenant, then 
ye shall be a peculiar kingdom of priests, and 
a holy nation.” In the most ancient and fa- 


mous Targum of Onkelos, this passage is 


“kings and priests.” Now I have no doubt 
this will be fulfilled in the day of that new 
covenant, in which the Lord hath promised to 
“put his laws into their hearts, and to write 
them in their minds ;” which is the day of the 
rising of the sun, when accordingly we find 
them designated as kings. And even we who 
have had faith given us to believe on Christ 
under the present dispensation, have the same 
style given unto us in the first chapter of the 
Apocalypse, ‘‘Unto him who loved us, and 
washed us from our sin in his own blood, and 
made us to be kings and priests unto our God,” 
and the saints in glory adopt the same style, add- 
ing to it, “and we shall reign upon the earth.” 

Philalethes—Some commentators have held 
from this passage that the kings of the east 
signify the risen saints. 

Aristo.—But in that case, to prepare the way of 
such would require no wasting away of the Eu- 
phrates, for when they come they will come in the 
glory and irresistible strength of the resurrec- 
tion, which flesh and blood can never withstand. 
Perhaps the strongest reason of all for using 


_this style of the Jewish people is derived from 


Daniel’s famous prophecy of the five monarchies, 
where the progress of the Lord’s providence is 


121 


set forth under the figuré of a great colossal 
image, whose head of gold was Nebuchadnezzar’s 
kingdom, and his breast and arms of silver, the 
Persian, and his belly and thighs of brass, the 
Greek, and his legs and tén toes of iron and 
clay, the Roman Monarchy in the ten gothic 
kingdoms. This statue is now in the toes; and 
im the battle of Armageddon, these, and all the 
rest of it, are ground into dust, by the smiting 
of the stone cut out without hands; after which 
it is said that the God of heaven shall set up 
a kingdom which shall never be destroyed ; and 
the kingdom shall not be left to other people, 
but it shall break in pieces and consume all 
these kingdoms, and it shall stand for ever. 
And who the rulers of this fifth kingdom are, 
we are told in the vision of the four beasts, 
which represent the same fourfold succession 
of brutal power; after which it is said, that the 
kingdom, and dominion, and greatness of the 
kingdoms of the whole heaven shall be given 
to the people of the saints of the Most High, 
whose kingdom is an everlasting kingdom: and 
again, the saints of the Most High shall take 
the kingdom, and shall possess the kingdom, 
even for ever ahd ever. This is the kingdom 
of God to which the Jews looked, and of which 
they enquired in the days of our Lord; to which 
they still look, and which shall be given them 
as surely as it is written every where in the 
Word of God. To them therefore belongs the 
style of kings, if it belonged to the Babylonian 
head of God, and to the Roman toes of Iron,

122 


the kings of the earth which now are in being; 
and this style which belongeth unto them it was 
proper to bestow upon them under the sixth 
vial, which is preparing for the destruction of 
the ten kingdoms, with all dominion and power 
under the whole heaven. When the possession 
was notified as about- to be removed, it was pro- 
per for the King of kings to notify also those 
into whose hands the possession was about to 
come. And when the kings of the earth, “and 
of the whole world, are preparing to be de- 
stroyed, it is proper to prepare the way for 
the kings who are to take the government of 
the world in hand. And to guard against mis- 
take, they are not called kings of any place,. 
nor with any possession, nor, as I think, kings 
from any place, but deriving their origin from 
an event ‘the rising of the sun,’ which all 
throughout Scripture is assigned both as the 
cause and the occasion of their coming to their 
universal and everlasting kingdom. 

=- For all these reasons I see no impropriety, 
but rather, on the other hand, much beauty and 
propriety, in taking this symbol, ‘‘kings from 
the rising of the sun,” to represent the Jewish 
people, who in that day shall be used by the 
Lord to trample upon, and tread down many. 
kings, as heretofore they did in the days of 
Joshua, when they came up to.possess the land 
of promise. They are kings of the earth by the

words,. Isa. xli. “ Who raised up the righteous.

man from the East, called him to his foot, gave

Holy One of Israel.” 

Anastasius.—Mede has also contended that 
‘‘the kings of the east” mean the Jews: which 
expression Mr. Faber has very properly cor- 
rected, by shewing that they are not “ Jews” in 
the strict sense of the word, namely, of the 
kingdom of Judah; but that it is the ten tribes 
who are signified by that. term. 

Philalethes.—Can you give me any additional 
proof that the ten tribes will be the instrument

Hoty Land, and the Eastern part of Christen-

I have already observed, that there are dna 
descriptions of kings mentioned in Rev. xvi. 
The kings of the east, the kings of the earth, 
and the kings of the whole world; of which we 
know, without any doubt, the precise import of. 
the latter, and have only to enquire the import 
of the former. The kings of the earth signify the 
ten kings of the papal apostacy, against whom 
especially the judgment of the great day of God 
Almighty is prepared ; and all powers and poten- 
tates who are in that same great day of discomfi- 
ture to be confederate against the Lord, and 
against his anointed; and to be, by Him, stripped 
of their abused dignities, in order that the earth 
and the fulness thereof, may pass into the hands 
of the great Son and Heir, who has been sitting 
now a long season expecting till his enemies be 
made his footstool; according to the decree de- 
clared of old, even from the day of his genera- 
tion, “ Ask of me, and I shall give thee the 
heathen for thine inheritance, and the uttermost 
parts of the earth for thy possession.” This 
confederacy of the kings, and princes, and 
people of the earth against Him, is always 


125 


given as the sign of the time of His installation 
into His long decreed throne, upon His holy hill 
of Zion: and in all the Prophets who make 
mention of these things, this great gathering 
and discomfiture of nations is represented as 
taking place at Jerusalem, and in the valley of 
Jehoshaphat, Joel iii. 1; Isa. lxvi. 13. 15, 16; 
Zech. xiv. 2. To the same great crisis of the 
destruction of all anti-christian power for ever 
from the earth, does the Ps. Ilxxxiii. refer, where 
all the nations and peoples then known, being 
ten in number, take crafty counsel together; 
against thy people, and consult against thy 
hidden ones, saying, ‘“‘ Come and let us cut them 
off from being a nation, that the name of Israel 
may be no more in remembrance. And the 
same mystery I take to be couched under 
Ezekielľs prophecy of Gog of the land of 
Magog, the prince of Ross, Meshech, and 
Fubal, who brought with him also Persia, 
Ethiopia, and Libya, Gomer and all his bands, 
the house of Togarmah of the North Quarter, 
and all his bands, and many peoples with them: 
This confederacy of all nations, under one Gog, 
comes up like a cloud against the children of 
Israel, dwelling securely in their own land, and 
the account of it follows immediately after the 
prophecy of their conversion and restoration : 
and the language in which the battle of Arma- 
geddon is described, is taken almost verbatim 
from Ezek. xxxix. 17—24. 

To any one who gives the least heed to ‘the 
holy prophets, and has an ear able to understand 


126 


‘plain ‘words, it is manifest from them, and a 
hundred other passages, that the kings and na- 
‘tions of the whole world are gathered together, 
to contend against, and utterly to make away 
with the Jewish people, already restored, or in 
the way of being restored, to their own land; 
their hatred to God expresses itself in a bloody 
purpose of exterminating his chosen and pecu- 
har people, for whom he has, in a striking way, 
interfered ; and, by mighty acts, restored to their 
inheritance ; and this, no doubt, is the mystery 
of the battle of the great day of God Almighty, 
to which the kings of the earth are gathered 
together into a place, called in the Hebrew 
tongue Armageddon. Little indeed is said in 
the Apocalypse concerning any other kings, 
save those of the earth, or apostate Gentile 
church, with which it hath chiefly to do.. As 
little is said of the Jewish people, for the sealed 
ones of Rev. vii. are emblematical of this nà- 
tion, which, because of its faithful protestation, 
is sealed during the time of the vials of wrath ; 
and yet enough is said to parallel and synchro- 
‘nize it with the ancient prophets who prophesied 
‘of that people. In chap. ii. ver. 7, it is written, 
** Behold he cometh with clouds, and every eye 
shall see him, and they also which pierced him, 
and all the kindreds of the earth shall wail be- 
cause of him.” Here, immediately before the 
wailing of all kindreds, or concomitant with 
it, he is manifested with clouds to those who 
pierced him, that is, to the Jewish people, which 
well agrees to what we have derived from the 


127 


‘other prophets. Now, it is under the sixth 
vial that we arrive at the notification of. his 
coming, “ Behold, I come as a thief:” as a thief, 
to denote the sleepy, heedless, unprovided con- 
dition in which he would take the Gentile church, 
to whom this call is particularly addressed ; and 
we should, therefore, expect that at this time 
also, he would be appearing to them that 
pierced him. Moreover, it is said of the beast 
that cometh out of the bottomless pit, the last 
head of the beast, and also of the seven, that he 
is to lead that grand antichristian confederacy 
-of nations, the Gog, as I take it, of Ezekiel’s 
prophecy, the Assyrian of Isaiah, and the Wil- 
ful King of Daniel’s; that he shall make war 
with the Lamb, (xvii. 14,) but the Lamb shall 
overcome him, for he is Lord of lords and King 
of kings, and they that are with him are called 
“ faithful and chosen.” Here are certain along 
with him in this great controversy, who are 
described to be called, or summoned, as it were, 
from afar, faithful in the midst of universal 
apostacy, and chosen to witness, and to execute, 
their judgments upon the enemies of the Lord. 
Which is very much akin to the language used 
of the same Jewish people, in the day the Lord 
calleth them, and appeareth for them. Now, if 
we can further shew that these kings of the 
east are the Jewish people, to which the analogy 
of all the prophets not a little predisposes the 
mind, there would be established a very great 
harmony amongst them, and a most comfortable ~ 
assurance to the church. | 


128 


These kings of the east must be brought for 
a different end than the kings of the earth, 
because their way is prepared by the Lord, 
whereas the way of the others is prepared by 
the three unclean spirits from the mouth of the 
dragon, and from the mouth of the beast, and 
the mouth of the false prophet. The way of 
the eastern kings is prepared by the angel of 
the Lord, who is commissioned for that end 
with a vial of consumption upon the waters of 
Euphrates. The Lord interferes, on their ac- 
count, to cut off a wicked power which would 
have hindered them. He exhausts those waters 
which had come up over the borders of Ema- 
nuel’s Land, which had overwhelmed the holy 
city, and drowned the holy people. He banks 
in the Euphrates stream, which, in the begin- 
ning of their troubles, in the days of Isaiah, 
had swelled out under the Assyrian, and hath 
never since ceased to vex all the Holy Land; 
yea, he smites them in his wrath, and they are 
consumed: and all this is to make way for cer- 
tain kings from the rising of the sun: and this 
is done at a time when all the kings of the 
earth and of the world are gathering to their 
destruction. Can it be, that these kings are 
likewise brought to their destruction? We 
would conclude otherwise from the language 
and machinery of the vial. Besides, they 
would be twice reckoned in the muster roll: 
once under the name of the kings of the east, 
and a second time under the name of the kings 
of the whole world ; in the former case, braught 


129 


up by God.preparing the way ; and in the second, 
instigated by the fury of an antichristian spirit. 
Now, this confusion and redundance is no where 
to be expected in Scripture, least of all in the 
Apocalypse, which is written with the utmost 
exactness. ae 

I observe, secondly, that if these kings of 
the east are understood amongst the potentates 
of the whole world, who come up to make war 
with the Lamb, and with his called, and faithful, 
and chosen companions, there were no need 
to dry up the waters of Euphrates, who would 
readily league and concur with them in any — 
such antichristian confederacy. I mean, there 
would be no use to consume the Turk, in order 
to make way for kings who are to be confede- 
rates with the Turk, in the same ungodly un- 
dertaking. They would not stand in their way 
at all, but rather be a furtherance to them. For 
if those kings of the east be the present po- 
tentates thereof, they are of the same religion 
and policy with the Turks, whose destruction 
would rather hinder than further their schemes. 
But, on the other hand, it would be a most 
necessary preparation for the return. of the 
tribes, because it is distinctly prophesied, that 
it should so take place before that event. In 
Is. xviii. where both their return, and the nation 
by whose help and protection it is to be accom- 
plished, is set forth, they are described as “a 
nation scattered and peeled, a nation wonderful 
from their beginning hitherto,” that is, up to 
that time: ** expecting, expecting, whose. land: 


130 


rivers have spoiled, or inundated.” Before 
these could be brought as a present unto the 
Lord of Hosts in Zion, it is necessary that 
those rivers, which have inundated and spoiled 
their land, should be dissipated, which is taking 
place now under the sixth vial. And if they 
be terrible up to that time, they must acquire 
that character by some acts of prowess, which 
may procure for them the headship of the tribes, 
and the title of kings of the east. The same thing 
is prophesied in Is. xxvii. 12; 13; “ and it shall 
come to pass in that day, that the Lord shall 
beat off from the channel of the river unto 
the stream of Egypt, and ye shall be gathered 
one by one, O ye children of Israel: and 
it shall come to pass in that day, that the 
great trumpet shall be blown, and they shall 
come which were ready to perish in the land 
of Assyria, and the outcasts in the land of 
Egypt, and shall worship the Lord in the holy 
mount, at Jerusalem.” This is the Lord’s 
threshing, so often described in the prophecy, 
and the bounds within which it is to take place: 
his barnfloor is between Euphrates and the 
river of Egypt, which is to be thoroughly clean- 
sed, and exhausted of its various people, in 
order to the bringing home of the captives of 
Israel, in Assyria, and in Egypt. And be- 
tween these two figures, threshing .a barnfloor, 
and evaporating waters, there is a great simila- 
rity in respect to the internal and universal cha- 
racter of the judgment. Now it is manifest, 
that these bounds are at present possessed by the 


131 


Turkish people, who therefore must be first 
threshed like the dust, and carried away of 
the wind, or consumed with evaporation before 
the captives are brought home. And when 
we find this done under the sixth vial, and 
the cause of it assigned to be for making way 
for a royal company out of the east, we should 
at once say, that, according to the prophets, 
this company must be the Jewish outcasts, 
gathered again into one. 

Thirdly, I observe, that if those kings from the 
east be not the Jews, then not only have we 
no mention made of them at all in this book, 
though a hint to that effect had been given 
in the first chapter, but we have no separation 
hor setting of them apart from the general con- 
gregation of the kings of the whole world, 
who are destroyed at Armageddon. For as 
hath been said, the sealed tribes are not to 
be taken literally of the Jews, but symbolically 
of a complete nation which had been separated 
from the apostate faction, and was - therefore 
defended against the judgments thereof. The 
Jews would thereby be wholly omitted, and the 
prophecy would not redeem its own promise, 
that “they who pierced him, should also be- 
hold him,” and which being read in Zech. 
is a look of godly sorrow, and penitent contri- 
tion; ‘they shall look on me whom they 
have pierced, and mourn as one that mourneth 
for a first born.” Instead of this conversion 
of the tribes in, or rather before the day of 
the great slaughter, we should have them passed 
over in total silence; yea, gathered along with 


132 


the rest into the slaughter-house of Armaged- 
don, whither it is said that the kings of the 
whole world are gathered. Whereas the trath 
is, that it is in controversy for Zion, and re- 
compence upon her enemies that the judgment 
of all nations takes place. But if we under- 
stand these kings from the east, to be the Jew- 
ish people, then all proceeds according to the 
course of all the prophecies. The Lord appears 
to them: their sun of righteousness arises upon 
them with healing of all their wounds in his 
wings, and they go forth, and they grow up 
like calves in the stalls: and being grown, 
and waxed mighty, they tread down the wicked, 
thresh them out with their feet, as the oxen 
do the grain, and they shall be ashes under 
the sole of their feet. Now the sixth vial; 
making preparation for destroying all the 
kings of the whole world, it was a very 
proper accompaniment to present unto us the 
Jews, under the figure of kings, who, in the 
time to come, were to exercise the kingdom 
afterwards. And thus is the promise held out 
at the beginning of the book fulfilled to them : 
and it is signified that he hath appeared to them 
in‘glory, and that they have come to his rising; 
and by his being installed king, they come 
up to defeat the antichristian combination of 
his‘enemies, and enter into the long promised 
kingdom of Messiah their Prince. 

' This will the more clearly appear, if we con- 
sider the whole emblem together, and the histo- 
rical fact in the times of old, which was ordained- 
for:the.type of it, and in the language of which 


133 


it-is. written. This Í have no doubt is the taking 
of Babylon by Cyrus, concerning which we have 
so many prophecies in the Old Testament. 
Babylon was ordained of old to be the city of 
the people’s captivity, and Euphrates to be the 
river by which they should sit and weep. Thi- 
ther were they led captive in the time of Nebu- 
chadnezzar, and after being brought through 
many trials, and having seen many deliverances, 
it was ordained by God that they should be de- 
livered by the hand of Cyrus, who being migh- 
tily strengthened of the Lord, came up at the 
time appointed, and laid siege to Babylon, but 
could not prevail against it, because of the in- 
credible height and thickness.of the walls, and 
massive solidity of its brazen gates. Through 
the midst of the city ran the river Euphrates, in 
breadth a full quarter of a mile, and in depth 
equal to the height of two men. The place 
being full of all manner of stores, was deemed 
impregnable, and was truly so, but for that 
which the Lord had prophesied, Isa. xliv. 22, 
saying, ‘‘ that saith to the deep be dry, and I 
will dry up thy river;” and again, Jer. li. 36, 
“I will take vengeance for them, and I will dry 
up her sea, and make her springs dry.” Di- 
rected, no doubt, by the Divine Spirit, which 
had thus foretold even the manner of Babylon’s 
downfall, Cyrus fell upon the most extraordi- 
nary expedient of turning the river Euphrates 
into another channel, which he cut for it, and so 
marching dry-shod down the bed into the city, 
and thence ascending through the gates of the 
streets leading to the river, which God had pro- 


134 


- mised to open uñto him, and they were left open 
in the confusion of the great feast, he made a- 
prey of it wholly, as it is written in Daniel; 
and in the first year of his reign in Babylon, he 
issued his decree for the rebuilding of the tem-. 
_ ple, under which decree Jerusalem was rebuilt, 
and certain of the captives of Babylon and Judah | 
returned unto the land of their fathers ; but the 
ten tribes returned not. pa 
Such was the event in the language of which: 
this and the following vial is written. Under 
this vial the Euphrates is represented as dried 
up, in order to prepare the way of certain kings 
from the rising of the sun; and in the next vial 
Babylon is destroyed. Now it is diligently to. 
be observed, that Cyrus did not destroy Baby- 
lon, but beautified it for the chief seat of his 
empire. His commission was to destroy her 
idolatrous king and his idols; and to set the 
captives of the Lord free to go up and build the 
city and the temple. For this end the river Eu- 
phrates was dried up before him, and the two- 
leaved gates opened before him; and for the 
same end I believe that the Euphrates is now 
drying up before these kings from the rising of 
the sun, that they may come up against the 
king who shall then be over Babylon, and de- 
stroy him, and set the captives of the Lord free 
from under his yoke. This Babylon is the 
Western Roman Empire, with its ten papal 
kingdoms, which shali then have one king over 
them, the great personal Antichrist of the latter 
days ; when the Lion of the tribe of Judah shall 
come up from the swellings of Jordan, bringing 


135 


along with :him- those kings who come to the- 
brightness of his rising; by whom Antichrist 
shall be discomfited as in Perazim, and in the 
valley of Gibeon, as Belshazzar was by Cyrus, 
and the Assyrian by the breath of the Angel 
of the Lord. Which being accomplished, the, 
seed of Israel, which were held captive under 
Babylon, that is, the remnant of the tribes of 
Judah and Benjamin, which have for 2000 years 
been as it were entombed alive in the bowels of 
Rome, shall be set free by thése kings from the 
rising of the sun, and the temple shall be re- 
built, and Jerusalem the blessed shall be reared 
up again in her beauty and her holiness, in her 
mighty strength and glorious magnificence ; 
which shall no more be termed Forsaken, neither 
shall her name any more be termed Desolate, 
&e. And here the parallel ends; for with the 
destruction of the mystical Babylon these kings 
from the rising of the sun having nothing at all 
to do, as Cyrus had nothing to do with the de- 
struction of the literal Babylon, which came 
not about till many centuries after. Not so, 
however, with the mystical Babylon, which 
is destroyed under the next vial, and that by 
her own ten horns, or kings, under the instiga- 
tion of antichrist, their leader, before he is him- 
self destroyed. By which I understand, that 
the papal apostacy is destroyed by him, in 
order to make way for his personal usurpation 
of the power of Babylon, which the drunken 
woman hereintofore only ruled: in which, having 
securely seated himself, he becomes the an- 


136 


titype of Sennacherib the Assyrian, and Bel- 
shazzar the Babylonian, seated in his temple, 
and worshipped there as God : oppressing all the 
people who will acknowledge only the true God. 

Philalethes.—But why do you consider this. 
action of Cyrus, as parallel with that under the | 
sixth vial? 

Aristo. — The language is manifestly the 
same; and if you will examine the prophecies 
which relate to Cyrus, as the subverter of the 
Babylonish power, and the redeemer of God’s 
people, you will find that event to include under 
it, and expressly to mention this last deliver- 
ance which shall be accomplished by the tribes 
of Israel. Is. xl.is a prophecy every way too 
large and capacious for Cyrus, though to Cyrus 
it be given by name, and doubtless for Cyrus 
in the first instance was written. For example, 
ver. 13, 14, “I have raised him up in righteous- 
ness, and I will direct his ways; he shall build 
my city, and he shall let go my captives, not 
for price nor reward, saith the Lord of Hosts; 
thus saith the Lord, the labour of Egypt, and 
merchandize of Ethiopia, and of the Sabeans, 
men of stature shall come over unto thee, and 
they shall be thine: they shall come after thee, 
in chains they shall come over, and they shall 
fall down unto thee, they shall make supplica- 
tion unto thee, saying, surely God is in thee ; 
and there is none else, there is no God.” This 
never was fulfilled of Cyrus, of whom it never 
could be said, with any propriety, that he was 
raised up in righteousness, being an idolater 


137 


from his birth even to his death: nor was the 
Jerusalem, whose building he set on foot, ever 
so triumphant or so honoured among the na- 
tions; but perpetually vexed, plundered, and 
destroyed ; and that which follows was still- 
less fulfilled, 16, 17. ‘‘'They‘shall be ashamed, 
and also confounded, -all of them; they shall 
go to confusion together that are: makers of 
idols; but Israel shall be saved in the Lord 
with an everlasting salvation; ye shall not be 
ashamed nor confounded, world without end.’’: 
Neither, on the one hand, have idolaters all 
been ashamed and confounded together, nor 
upon the other hand, hath Israel been saved 
in the Lord with an everlasting salvation. And 
-the same may be said of all that strain of 
prophecy which occurs from the xlvth to the 
xlixth chapter, concerning the destruction of 
Babylon, namely, that Cyrus and his army 
are not able to do, nor any human power is able 
to do, the things which are foretold, and were 
foreshadowed under the great historical act 
of Cyrus, the shepherd of the Lord. This — 
conviction, however strong, would not have borne 
out the conclusion, that he was typical of 
Christ, and his army of the restored tribes 
of Israel, but which may be gathered from 
the additional light cast upon the subject, by 
Jer l. and li., which chapters are wholly dedi- 
cated to the overthrow of Babylon. Im the 
-former of these chapters, after the Lord had 
sufficiently described the northern nations, 
which were to be the instruments of the destruc-

tion of the literal Babylon, and set forth her

her gold cup, ver. 7, even she that dwelleth upon

the former of all things; and Israel is the rod

name. Thou art my battle-axe, and weapons 
of war; for with thee will I break in pieces the

his rider; and with thee will I break in pieces 
the chariot and his rider ; and with thee also will 
I break in pieces man and woman; and with thee

and the maid; and I will also break in pieces 
with thee the shepherd and his flock; and

and his yoke of oxen; and with thee will I break 
an pieces captains and rulers; and I will render 
unto Babylon, and to all the inhabitants of 
Chaldea, all their evil which they have done in 
Zion, in your sight, saith the Lord.” 

Than which passage nothing can be more 
demonstrative of the use which the Lord shall 
make of Jacob his portion in the overwhelming 
of the last king of Babylon, and the redemption

of his people from her bondage. This shews us 
of whom the northern nations, with Cyrus at 
their head, were typical, even of the tribes under 
the leading of Him whose name is the Lord of 
Hosts. The same inference is to be derived 
from Isaiah xiii., which consists of two parts, 
the universal overthrow of nations in the day of 
the Lord, and the particular overthrow of Baby- 
ton by the Medes: which, yet, are not separate 
subjeets but one subject; the thing typified and 
the type; the general rule and the individual . 
example. Now those who are to do in the 
general what the Medes did in the particular, 
are thus described, ver. 2—5.; “ Lift ye up a 
banner upon the high mountain, exalt the voice 
unto them, shake the hand that they may go 
into the gates of the nobles. I have com- 
manded my sanetified ones, I have also called 
my mighty ones for mine anger, even them that 
rejoice in my highness. The noise of a mul- 
titude in the mountains, like as of a great 
people; a tumultuous noise of the kingdoms 
of nations gathered together ; the Lord of Hosts 
mustereth the host of the battle: they come 
from a far country, from the end of heaven, even 
the Lord, and the weapons of his indignation, to 
destroy the whole land.” These names, “my 
sanctified ones,’ “even them that rejoice 
in my highness,” can apply only to the 
Jewish tribes; and if any will apply them to 
the idolatrous Medes and Persians, I ask where 
did they do these mighty and wonderful things 
which are described, ver. 7—14; or were these

things accomplished in their time, or are they 
yet accomplished? Surely they have not yet 
been accomplished upon the earth, and therefore 
the instruments by whom they are to be accom- 
plished have not yet been summoned to the 
work; and that which the Medes did was only 
‘the sign and assurance of it upon that wicked 
city, which in those days did most grievously 
oppress the people of the Lord. But to put it 
beyond the possibility of doubt that it is by the 
_ people of Israel the Lord is to accomplish the 
destruction of the powers of Babylon, after — 
these have of themselves destroyed the drunken 
and idolatrous woman, which long sat upon 
them, and intoxicated them with her cup, we © 
finally refer to the famous prophecy which is 
written in Mic.x.6. “ In that day,” i.e. the 
day of the exaltation of the mountain of the 
house of the Lord, ‘ saith the Lord, will I as- 
semble her that halteth, and I will gather her 
that is driven out, and her that I have afflicted.” — 
This latter must be the ten tribes, who were driven 
far away, and were sorely afflicted; and that 
former, therefore, must be the tribes of Judah and 
Benjamin, which were wounded, and imprisoned, 
but not utterly ejected and cast out. “ And 
I will make her that halteth a remnant,” which 
we now behold her, ‘ and her that was cast off 
a strong nation ;” which we do not yet, but soon 
shall behold accomplished, of the ten tribes; 
“ and the Lord shall reign over them in Mount 
Zion, from henceforth and for ever,” over Israel 
and Judah now happily united in one, and

settled in their own land. ‘“ And unto thee, O

the first dominion ; the kingdom shall come to 
the daughter of Jerusalem:” that is, the do-

siah and to the daughter of Jerusalem, the 
king and the queen in the day of their es- 
pousals. With the argument of this comfort- 
able prophecy, he then proceeds to reason with 
the daughter of Zion, under her sore trials and 
griefs, saying, “ why dost thou cry aloud ? is

rished? for pains have taken thee as a woman 
in travail: be in pain and labour to bring forth, 
O Daughter of Zion, like a woman in travail : 
for now shalt thou go forth out of the city, and 
thou shalt dwell im the field:” that is, she 
should have to go a second time out of human 
dwellings and lodge in the tented field, accord-

and bring her into the wilderness, and speak 
comfortably to her ;” and what shall follow upon

to Babylon; there shalt thou be delivered ;

of thine enemies ;” and then follows the gather-

say let her be defiled, and let her eye look

upon Zion.” And what follows this their daily 
attempt to pollute and defile the betrothed wife

thoughts of the Lord, neither understand they 
his counsels, for he shall gather them as the 
sheaves into the floor: arise, and thresh, O 
daughter of Zion, for I will make thine horn 
iron, and i will make thy hoofs brass, and thou 
shalt beat in pieces many people: and I will 
consecrate their gain unto the Lord, and their 
substance unto the Lord of the whole earth.” 
This I conceive to be the fullest and clearest 
elucidation of the whole mystery, in which I 
perceive that by the hands of the strong nation 
that was cast out, which is Israel, the halting 
one, which is Judah, to whom the first dominion 
is promised, shall be delivered out of the 
thraldom of Babylon: after which, these two 
united again in one, under David their king, 
_ shall be as a mighty ox, with horns of iron, 
and hoofs of brass, threshing the sheaves of 
the barnfloor, and grinding them to powder ; or, 
as it is written by this same prophet, “and 
the remnant of Jacob shail be among the Gen- 
tiles, in the.midst of many people, as a lion 
` amongst the beasts of the forest, as a young 
lion among the flocks of sheep; who, if he go 
through both, treadeth down and teareth in 
pieces, and none can deliver;” or, after the

hearth of fire amongst the wood, or like a

Anastasius.—Surely we may say, “there is 
none like unto the God of Jeshurun, who rideth 
upon the heaven in thy help, and in his excel- 
lency on the sky: the Eternal God is thy re- 
fuge, and underneath are the everlasting arms : 
and he shall thrust out the enemy from before 
thee, and shall say, ‘Destroy them.’ Israel 
then shail dwell in safety alone ; the fountain of 
Jacob shall be upon a land of corn and wine ; 
also his heavens shall drop down dew. Happy 
art thou, O Israel! who is like unto thee, O 
people saved by the Lord, the shield of thy 
help, and who is the sword of thy excellency! 
and thine enemies shall be subdued unto thee, 
and thou shalt tread upon their high places.” 
Deut. xxxiii. 25—29.

CONVERSATION THE TENTH. 


What is revealed concerning the Manner of the Restoration of 
the Two, and of the Ten Tribes? 


Philalethes.—Since it seems clear, from our 
last conversation, that the Jews will be the in- 
struments in the Lord’s hand of inflicting final 
judgment on the nations at Armageddon, it is 
probable that the particulars respecting their 
restoration are given with great minuteness. 

Evander.—An opinion has been entertained 
by some persons, that believers among the 
Christian nations are descendants of the ten 
tribes, and several passages of Scripture have 
been brought together in order to support that 
idea. 

Anastasius —I have seen three papers in 
manuscript, drawn up by three persons, each of 
whom is unknown to the other, all maintaining 
the same position, but supported by reasoning 
derived from entirely different sources. 

Philalethes— The coincidence of three inde- 
pendent testimonies is so remarkable, that I 
should like to hear how this view is supported. 

Anastasius.—1 have two objections to laying 
it before you; first, that I do not understand it 
well myself; secondly, the little I do under- 
stand seems to be so ill borne out by Scripture, 
that it would be unprofitable to make the state- 
ment without examining its foundations, and 


145 


overthrowing it, where it was untenable: but this 
would take much time, and prevent our hearing 
what Aristo can tell us upon this very difficult 
subject, into which I know he has inquired 
deeply, and which, when considered in con- 
nexion with the day of wrath of Almighty God, 
is of the very greatest practical importance. 
Philemon.—There are not only fallacies in 
many parts of the view to which Evander 
has referred, that are easy of detection, but 
there are also some objections to be brought 
against its basis, which are quite fatal; for ex- 
ample, there is nothing in Scripture to intimate 
that the ten tribes would be blessed with the 
saving knowledge of Christ before the other 
two; and at the time the Israelites are said to 
be gathered into their own land, they are termed 
“outcasts,” which is not likely to be the case 
if they were already in the covenant of grace ; 
neither would there be much advantage to them in 
the restoration of their nation at the millennium, 
seeing they would at that time be themselves in 
glory, and consequently care little who reigned 
in Judea. I have found great difficulty in recon- 
ciling the apparent contradictions in the manner 
of their restoration: thus in Jer. 1. 4, it is writ- 
ten, “ In those days, and in that time, saith the 
Lord, the children of Israel shall come, they 
and the children of Judah together, going, and 
weeping : they shall go and seek the Lord their 
God.” In iii. 18, they are also represented as 
going up together; but in Zech. xii. 7, it is said, 


146 
“the Lord also shall save the tents of Judah 
first; and in Isaiah they are foretold to “return 
with songs of joy on their heads.” 

Anastasius.—1 think the word rendered first, ` 
in Zech. xii. 7, mws», means rather, “in the 
first or foremost place;”’ which rendering you 
will find justified by Parkhurst. The Septua- 
gint version, xa0we az’ apync, does a greater 
‘violence to the original, but shews that these 
translators had no idea the word implied a re- 
storation prior in time. But the strongest evi- 
dence is the line of argument pursued in the 
context, which is clearly relating to the re- 
establishment of the house, and dynasty, of 
Judah and David, as of old, and in no way 
referring to their priority of time of arriving in 
the Holy Land. And in this sense I would also 
apply the passage in Mic. iv. 8: “ And thou, 
O tower of the flock, the strong hold of the 
daughter of Zion, unto thee shall it come, even 
the first dominion.” 

Thraso——Another answer to the opinion men- 
tioned by Zvander is, that the Jews are always 
spoken of to be restored as Jews ; but if they 
had been Christians, it is obvious they are no 
longer Jews. As the deliverance from Babylon 
of old was partial, and that from Egypt total, 
so whenever these subjects are alluded to with 
reference to their future deliverance, that from 
the former signifies a partial, and that from the 
latter a complete, restoration. I believe you 
will find that the words translated “ north coun- 


147 
try,” in Jer. xvi. 14; xxiii. 8, mean the “land 
of hiding ;” and the passage altogether applies 
to the lost, and not to the scattered tribes. 

Philalethes.—Have you followed up this sub- 
ject, Aristo, in connexion with what you told us 
respecting the destruction of the Jews at the 
battle of Armageddon ? 

Aristo.—VYes, I have; and I have found it one 
of the most difficult: that I ever examined: but’ 
I will tell you as much as I have been permitted 
to see of this mystery. In our conversation on 
the battle of Armageddon, it clearly appeared, 
from the concurrent testimony of all the pro- 
phets, that the captivity of the holy nation, 
which shall be found in Babylon against the 
day of the Lord, shall be delivered out of her 
thraldom by means of a powerful people, even 
kings from the east, which are also of the tribes 
of Israel. It next occurs, then, to inquire what 
tribes these are. And seeing that the captive 
tribes in the mystical Babylon are Judah and 
Benjamin, as it was heretofore in the literal 
Babylon, it seems to follow as of necessary con- 
sequence, that the tribes which deliver them 
must be the long lost ten tribes which were 
carried away beyond Assyria into the north 
country: and that these are the antitypes of 
Cyrus, and his many kings, who came also from 
Cush, and Assyria, and from Media, and from 
Persia, to deliver the tribes of Benjamin and 
Judah out of Babylon. 

Concerning the captivity of Israel, it is to be 
borne in mind, that it was completed in the days 


148 


of Hezekiah king of Judah, by Sennacherib, king © 
of Assyria, full six score years before the cap- 
tivity of Judah was effected by Nebuchadnezzar, 
king of Babylon; and that the former were 
taken away into the north land, scattered over 
the cities of Media, while the latter were carried - 
away to Babylon, and planted there in the 
towns and cities round about. So that as well by 
a long distance of time, as long distance of place, 
were the two captivities parted far asunder: 
and when Cyrus granted permission to them to 
return, you will find, by consulting the book of | 
Ezra, that those who availed themselves of the 
privilege, were all of the towns of Judah and 
Benjamin, except some of the Levites, which 
were scattered among all the tribes, for the 
right service of God. These multiplied and 
replenished the land until the time of our Lord 
and his apostles, when their city and temple 
were utterly destroyed by the Romans, and the 
remnant of them scattered abroad amongst the 
nations of the West, as we behold them at the 
present time. Of those who remained at Baby- 
lon, and would not return, the descendants are 
found to this day scattered over the East, in 
Mesopotamia, and Persia, and Arabia, and 
Turkey ; but of the ten tribes we are altogether 
ignorant; and the most learned of the Jews 
themselves know nothing, save that they are — 
somewhere to be found in the north-east of 
Asia. Thus we have in the West, Judah dis- 
persed chiefly over the Roman Empire, captives 
under the mystical Babylon, while Israel in 


149 


the East, is somewhere existing beyond the 
bounds thereof. This of itself would lead us 
to believe, that the kings from the rising of the 
sun are, if they belong to Israel at all, of the ten 
tribes who exist somewhere in the eastern parts 
of the world. To this distinction a passage 
in Isaiah xiv. I think, makes allusion: when. 
speaking of Cyrus, it is said, ver. 4, “ For Jacob 
my servant’s sake, and Israel mine elect, I have 
even called thee by thy name—I guided thee, 
though thou hast not known me, that they may 
know, from the rising of the sun, and from the 
West, that there is none besides me:”’ for this 
stands in no connexion with the heathen, but 
with the tribes of Israel only. However this 
may be, it is certain, that, ever since the time of 
Moses, the Lord hath foretold the diversity of 
stern and severe discipline to which he was 
about to subject the tribes which he had 
chosen. . | 

Philalethes.—I remember that Philemon point- 
ed out the passage in Deuteronomy, to which 
I suppose you allude, in our conversation on the 
Times of the Jews. 

Aristo.—Yes; that is the passage to which I 
refer. The sin of Israel was different from that 
of Judah, being rebellion in government, and 
infidelity in religion; whereas that of Judah 
was corruption in religion, and confederacy 
with the kings of the earth; into which diversity 
of evil courses they were permitted of the Lord 
to fall away, that he might shew forth the diver- 
sity of their two sins, and the diversity of their 


150 


several punishments: to the former, as being. 
the most heinous, he decreed instant cutting off, 
and long and sore rejection, with no benefit of 
that Saviour whom they had rejected, in rejeet- 
_ ing the house of David: while to the latter, he 
decreed 70 years captivity, and after their resto- 
ration, a succession of severe chastisements with 
the first presentation of Messiah and overtures 
of his Gospel ; honouring them to lay the founda- 
tion of the chureh. This diversity of judgment 
is distinctly announced in the first chapter of 
Hosea, who was raised up to prophecy to Israel 
a few years before the captivity, ver. 6; “ And 
God said, call his name Lo-ruhamah, for I will 
no more have mercy upon the house of Israel ; 
but I will utterly take them away; but I will 
have mercy upon the house of Judah, and will 
save them by the Lord their Ged, and will not 
save them by bow, nor by sword, nor by battle, 
by horses, nor by horsemen.” Which was ful- 
filled, when Sennacherib, the scourge of Israel, 
who swept them away, was broke before Jeru- 
salem with all his mighty host, by the breath 
of the angel of destruction. But in the very 
‘same chapter, it is thus said of the children of. 
israel, upon whom at that time the Lord had 
no mercy, “ yet the number of the children of 
Israel shall be as the sand of the sea, which 
cannot be measured nor numbered; and it shall 
come to pass, that in the place where it was 
said unto them, ye were not my people, there 
it shall be said unto them, ye are the sons of 
the living God. Then shall the children of 


151 


Judah, and the children of Israel be gathered 
together, and appoint themselves one head, and 
they shall come up out of the land, for great 
shall be the day of Jezreel.” In the next 
chapter of that same prophet, who, with Amos, 
may be considered as having performed for 
Israel that same office of instant expostulation, 
which Jeremiah afterwards performed for Judah, 
we have the manner of their restoration in the 
latter days, particularly and most effectually 
described, concluding in these words, “ Į will 
have merey upon her that had not obtained 
mercy, and I will say to those which were not 
my people, Thou art my people, and they shall 
say thou art my God ;” and in the third chapter, 
the time of this conversion and visitation of 
Israel, is given to be the time of David the 
king; that is, the time of the setting up again 
of the tabernacle of David, or of the coming of the 
true King David from the root of Jesse. “For 
the children of Israel shall abide many days 
without a king, and without a prince, and with- 
out a sacrifice, and without an image, and with- 
out an ephod, and without teraphim ; afterwards 
shall the children of Israel return and seek the 
Lord their God, and David the king, and shall 
fear the Lord and his goodness in the latter 
days.” So much is written with certainty con- 
cerning the restoration of these ten tribes in 
the latter days, and their re-union with Judah, 
under one king, even David. 

Philalethés.—But what part is Israel to take in 
the redemption of Judah? 


152 


Aristo.—I begin with the blessing pronounced. 
by Jacob upon Joseph, who was the father of 
two of the tribes, Ephraim and Manasseh,’ 
whereof the former was the strictest of all the 
ten, and stands in Scripture generally as the 
` representative of them all, even as Judah repre- 
sents the other two. Now, in blessing Joseph, 
the head of these tribes, the Patriarch Jacob 
thus expresses himself, Gen. xlix. 22, “ Joseph 
is a fruitful bough, even a fruitful bough by a 
well; whose branches run over the wall.” This 
refers to the exceeding populousness of the na- 
tions proceeding from him, ver. 23; “ The 
archers have sorely grieved him, and shot at 
him, and hated him.” This, I think, refers to 
their present sore and cruel treatment, which 
they are somewhere enduring, and the lan- 
guage is derived, I think, from the envious darts 
which were directed against him, and the sore 
straits to which he was brought in the land of 
Egypt; and would permit our using the deli- 
verance which he wrought for his father’s house 
there, as an emblem of some future deliverance, 
which he is yet to work in Israel ; but without 
resting on this, let us proceed to ver. 24; “ But 
his bow abode in strength, and the arms of his 
hands were made strong by the hands of the 
mighty God of Jacob; from thence is the Shep- 
herd and Stone of Israel.” This cannot be 
understood otherwise than of a future event, 
for of Joseph’s descendants no such thing has 
yet been fulfilled, except in the case of Joshua, 
who is referred to by Paul to the Hebrews, as 


155 


atype of the true Joshua. But subsequently 
his bow was broken by the Assyrian, and 
therefore did not abide in strength; his hands 
were not made strong by the mighty God of 
Jacob, but were bound and shackled by the 
Assyrians: and from him has not yet pro- 
ceeded the Shepherd, the Stone of Israel, which 
names can alone pertain to Christ, who is the 
Shepherd, against whom the sword of the Lord 
did heretofore awake, whom it smote, scattering 
the sheep, Zech. xiii.; but of whom it is promised 
again in Mic. v., after his birth in Bethlehem 
Ephratah, and his giving them up for a while, 
that “he shall stand and feed them in the 
strength of the Lord, in the majesty of the 
name of the Lord his God: and they shall 
abide ; for now shall he be great unto the ends 
of the earth; and this man shall be the peace, 
when the Assyrian shall come into the land, 
and when he shall tread in our palaces; then 
shall we raise against him seven shepherds and 
eight principal men; and they shall waste 
the land of Assyria with the sword, and the 
land of Nimrod in the entrances thereof; then ` 
will he deliver us from the Assyrian when he 
cometh into our land, and when he treadeth 
within our borders.” On this time of the de- 
struction of the Assyrian, or personal Anti- 
christ, and of Babylon his dominion, when the 
Lord asks, Is. |. 44, ‘“‘ Who is a chosen man 
that I may appoint over her? for who is like 
me? and who shall appoint me the time? and 
who is that Shepherd that will stand before
